# Input comparison: original preprocessed txt vs FLOORED copies

Computed from `candidate_floors.csv` via `write_floors.py` (dry-run logic -- identical whether or not the FLOORED.txt has been written to disk yet). See `write_floors.py` for the exact write algorithm.

**FLOORED.txt status at report time:** BAU: written, INV: written, OPT: written, VGB: written

Rule applied: `final_floor = max(existing_floor_in_original_txt, candidate_floor_PJ)`. A floor is only ever raised, never lowered -- legitimate 2023-2026 fleet floors (e.g. MEX ~830 PJ) are protected.

## Summary: BAU vs OPT

| | BAU | OPT |
|---|---|---|
| Plants floored | 51 | 51 |
| Tech-year floor rows (new+raised) | 961 | 1017 |

**BAU** total forced-generation floor across all years: 22,555.3 PJ over 24 years (~939.8 PJ/yr average).

**OPT** total forced-generation floor across all years: 37,277.2 PJ over 24 years (~1,553.2 PJ/yr average).

This gap in forced-generation floor (fossil dispatch guaranteed by construction) is the expected driver of scenario separation between BAU and OPT once both are re-solved: both scenarios floor the same 51 fossil-fleet techs (residual+forced basis), but OPT holds a flat, higher committed CF through 2050 and forces ~2.9x more fossil capacity, while BAU converges with OPT through 2030 then declines toward a keep-warm minimum by 2050.

## Feasibility gate

All candidate floors across all processed scenarios passed the feasibility gate (`feasibility.feasible_floor`). None were skipped.
